</p>
<div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">Belgian Roman Catholic priest and saint (1840–1889)</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For other people with similar names, see <a href="/wiki/Father_Damien_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Father Damien (disambiguation)">Father Damien (disambiguation)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Damien_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Saint Damien (disambiguation)">Saint Damien (disambiguation)</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Peter_Damian" title="Peter Damian">Peter Damian</a>.</div>

src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Father_Damien%27s_signature_1880.svg/200px-Father_Damien%27s_signature_1880.svg.png" decoding="async" width="200" height="36" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/Father_Damien%27s_signature_1880.svg/300px-Father_Damien%27s_signature_1880.svg.png 1.5x, /></a></span><br />Signature of Father Damien</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Father Damien</b> or <b>Saint Damien of Molokai</b>, <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Sacred_Hearts_of_Jesus_and_Mary" title="Congregation of the Sacred Hearts of Jesus and Mary">SS.CC.</a> or <b>Saint Damien De Veuster</b> (<a href="/wiki/Dutch_language" title="Dutch language">Dutch</a>: <i lang="nl">Pater Damiaan</i> or <i><span title="Dutch-language text"><i lang="nl">Heilige Damiaan van Molokai</i></span></i>; 3 January 1840 – 15 April 1889),<sup id="cite_ref-aocgov_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aocgov-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> born <b>Jozef De Veuster</b>, was a <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_(term)" title="Roman Catholic (term)">Roman Catholic</a> <a href="/wiki/Priesthood_(Catholic_Church)" class="mw-redirect" title="Priesthood (Catholic Church)">priest</a> from Belgium and member of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Sacred_Hearts_of_Jesus_and_Mary" title="Congregation of the Sacred Hearts of Jesus and Mary">Congregation of the Sacred Hearts of Jesus and Mary</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-cathency3_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cathency3-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> a <a href="/wiki/Missionary" title="Missionary">missionary</a> <a href="/wiki/Religious_institute" title="Religious institute">religious institute</a>. He was recognized for his ministry, which he led from 1873 until his death in 1889, in the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hawaii" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Hawaii">Kingdom of Hawaiʻi</a> to people with <a href="/wiki/Leprosy" title="Leprosy">leprosy</a> (Hansen's disease), who lived in government-mandated medical quarantine in a <a href="/wiki/Kalaupapa_Leprosy_Settlement" class="mw-redirect" title="Kalaupapa Leprosy Settlement">settlement</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Kalaupapa,_Hawaii" title="Kalaupapa, Hawaii">Kalaupapa</a> Peninsula of <a href="/wiki/Molokai" title="Molokai">Moloka&#x2bb;i</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Tayman_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tayman-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During this time, he taught the Catholic faith to the people of Hawaii. Father Damien also cared for the patients and established leaders within the community to build houses, schools, roads, hospitals, and churches. He dressed residents' ulcers, built a reservoir, made coffins, dug graves, shared pipes, and ate <a href="/wiki/Poi_(food)" title="Poi (food)">poi</a> with them, providing both medical and emotional support.
</p><p>After eleven years of caring for the physical, spiritual, and emotional needs of those in the <a href="/wiki/Leper_colony" title="Leper colony">leper colony</a>, Father Damien contracted leprosy. He continued with his work despite the infection but finally succumbed to the disease on 15 April 1889. Father Damien also had tuberculosis which worsened his condition, but some believe that the reason he volunteered in the first place was due to his illness of tuberculosis.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Father Damien has been described as a "<a href="/wiki/Martyr_of_charity" title="Martyr of charity">martyr of charity</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-SSCCBio_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SSCCBio-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Damien De Veuster is venerated as a <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">saint</a> in the Catholic Church. In the <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican Communion</a> and other Christian denominations, Damien is considered the spiritual patron for leprosy and outcasts. Father Damien Day, 15 April, the day of his death, is also a minor statewide holiday in Hawaii. Father Damien is the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Honolulu" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Honolulu">Diocese of Honolulu</a> and of <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawaii</a>.
</p><p>Father Damien was <a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">canonized</a> by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> on 11 October 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-CNA_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CNA-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-VR_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VR-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Libert_H._Boeynaems" title="Libert H. Boeynaems">Libert H. Boeynaems</a>, writing in the <i><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Encyclopedia" title="Catholic Encyclopedia">Catholic Encyclopedia</a>,</i> calls him "the Apostle of the Lepers."<sup id="cite_ref-Boeynaems_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boeynaems-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> Damien De Veuster's feast day is 10 May.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_life">Early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Father Damien was born Jozef ("Jef") De Veuster, the youngest of seven children and fourth son of the <a href="/wiki/Flemish_people" title="Flemish people">Flemish</a> corn merchant Joannes Franciscus ("Frans") De Veuster and his wife Anne-Catherine ("Cato") Wouters in the village of <a href="/wiki/Tremelo" title="Tremelo">Tremelo</a> in <a href="/wiki/Flemish_Brabant" title="Flemish Brabant">Flemish Brabant</a> in rural Belgium on 3 January 1840. His older sisters Eugénie and Pauline became nuns, and his older brother Auguste (Father Pamphile) joined the Congregation of the Sacred Hearts of Jesus and Mary (Picpus Fathers). Jozef was forced to quit school at age 13 to work on the family farm.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> His father sent him to a college at <a href="/wiki/Braine-le-Comte" title="Braine-le-Comte">Braine-le-Comte</a> to prepare for a commercial profession, but as a result of a mission given by the <a href="/wiki/Redemptorists" title="Redemptorists">Redemptorists</a> in 1858, Joseph decided to pursue a religious vocation.<sup id="cite_ref-Boeynaems_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boeynaems-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Jozef entered the novitiate of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_of_the_Sacred_Hearts_of_Jesus_and_Mary" title="Congregation of the Sacred Hearts of Jesus and Mary">Fathers of the Sacred Heart of Jesus and Mary</a> at Louvain and took in religion the name of Damien, presumably after the first <a href="/wiki/Saints_Cosmas_and_Damian" title="Saints Cosmas and Damian">Saint Damian</a>, a fourth-century physician and martyr.<sup id="cite_ref-colp_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-colp-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> He was admitted to the religious profession on 7 October 1860. 
</p><p>His superiors thought that he was not a good candidate for the priesthood because he lacked education. However, he was not considered unintelligent. Because he learned <a href="/wiki/Latin" title="Latin">Latin</a> well from his brother, his superiors decided to allow him to become a priest. During his religious studies, Damien prayed daily before a picture of St. <a href="/wiki/Francis_Xavier" title="Francis Xavier">Francis Xavier</a>, patron of missionaries, to be sent on a mission.<sup id="cite_ref-canvas_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-canvas-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-OW_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OW-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Three years later when his brother Father Pamphile (Auguste) could not travel to Hawaiʻi as a missionary because of illness, Damien was allowed to take his place.<sup id="cite_ref-NPS_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NPS-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Mission_in_Hawaii">Mission in Hawaii</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Mission in Hawaii">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg/160px-Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg" decoding="async" width="160" height="228" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg/240px-Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg/320px-Father_Damien_in_1878.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="1423" /></a><figcaption>Father Damien in 1873 before he sailed for Hawaii</figcaption></figure>
<p>On 19 March 1864, Damien arrived at <a href="/wiki/Honolulu_Harbor" title="Honolulu Harbor">Honolulu Harbor</a> on <a href="/wiki/Oahu" title="Oahu">Oʻahu</a>. He was ordained into the priesthood on 21 May 1864, at what is now the <a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_Our_Lady_of_Peace" class="mw-redirect" title="Cathedral of Our Lady of Peace">Cathedral of Our Lady of Peace</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1865, Damien was assigned to the <a href="/wiki/Sacred_Heart_Catholic_Church_in_Hawi" class="mw-redirect" title="Sacred Heart Catholic Church in Hawi">Catholic Mission in North Kohala</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Hawaii_(island)" title="Hawaii (island)">island of Hawai&#x2bb;i</a>. While he was serving in several parishes on Oʻahu, the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hawaii" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Hawaii">Kingdom of Hawaiʻi</a> was struggling with a labor shortage and a public health crisis.<sup id="cite_ref-moblo_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-moblo-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Many of the <a href="/wiki/Native_Hawaiians" title="Native Hawaiians">Native Hawaiian</a> parishioners had high mortality rates due to infectious diseases such as leprosy (from which he later died), <a href="/wiki/Smallpox" title="Smallpox">smallpox</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cholera" title="Cholera">cholera</a>, influenza, syphilis, and <a href="/wiki/Whooping_cough" title="Whooping cough">whooping cough</a>, brought to the <a href="/wiki/Hawaiian_Islands" title="Hawaiian Islands">Hawaiian Islands</a> by foreign traders, sailors and immigrants. Thousands of Hawaiians died of such diseases, to which they had no acquired <a href="/wiki/Immunity_(medical)" title="Immunity (medical)">immunity</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It is believed that <a href="/wiki/Chinese_immigration_to_Hawaii" title="Chinese immigration to Hawaii">Chinese workers</a> carried <a href="/wiki/Leprosy" title="Leprosy">leprosy</a> (later known as Hansen's disease) to the islands in the 1830s and 1840s. At that time, leprosy was thought to be highly contagious and was incurable. In 1865, out of fear of this contagious disease, Hawaiian King <a href="/wiki/Kamehameha_V" title="Kamehameha V">Kamehameha V</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Legislature_of_the_Kingdom_of_Hawaii" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislature of the Kingdom of Hawaii">Hawaiian Legislature</a> passed the "Act to Prevent the Spread of Leprosy." This law quarantined the lepers of Hawaii, requiring the most serious cases to be moved to a settlement colony of <a href="/wiki/Kalawao,_Hawaii" title="Kalawao, Hawaii">Kalawao</a> on the eastern end of the Kalaupapa peninsula on the island of <a href="/wiki/Molokai" title="Molokai">Moloka&#x2bb;i</a>. Later the settlement of <a href="/wiki/Kalaupapa,_Hawaii" title="Kalaupapa, Hawaii">Kalaupapa</a> was developed. <a href="/wiki/Kalawao_County" class="mw-redirect" title="Kalawao County">Kalawao County</a>, where the two villages are located, is separated from the rest of Moloka&#x2bb;i by a steep mountain ridge. From 1866 through 1969, about 8,000 Hawaiians were sent to the Kalaupapa peninsula for medical quarantine.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Royal Board of Health initially provided the quarantined people with food and other supplies, but it did not have the workforce and resources to offer proper health care.<sup id="cite_ref-Boeynaems_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boeynaems-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> According to documents of that time, the Kingdom of Hawaiʻi did not intend for the settlements to be <a href="/wiki/Penal_colony" title="Penal colony">penal colonies</a>. Still, the Kingdom did not provide enough resources to support them.<sup id="cite_ref-Tayman_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tayman-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> The Kingdom of Hawaii had planned for the lepers to be able to care for themselves and grow their crops. However, due to the effects of leprosy and the peninsula's local environmental conditions, this was impractical.
</p><p>By 1868, according to the <i>Catholic Encyclopedia</i> (1911), "Drunken and lewd conduct prevailed. The easy-going, good-natured people seemed wholly changed."<sup id="cite_ref-cathency2_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cathency2-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-critic_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-critic-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mission_on_Molokai">Mission on Molokai</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Mission on Molokai">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-More_citations_needed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Father_Damien" title="Special:EditPage/Father Damien">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>&#32;in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Father+Damien%22">"Father Damien"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Father+Damien%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Father+Damien%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Father+Damien%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Father+Damien%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Father+Damien%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">April 2017</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>While Bishop <a href="/wiki/Louis_D%C3%A9sir%C3%A9_Maigret" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis Désiré Maigret">Louis Désiré Maigret</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_vicariate" title="Apostolic vicariate">vicar apostolic</a> of the Honolulu diocese, believed that the lepers needed a Catholic priest to assist them, he realized that this assignment had high risk. He did not want to send any one person "in the name of obedience." After much prayer, four priests volunteered to go, among them Father Damien. The bishop planned for the volunteers to take turns in rotation assisting the inhabitants.
</p><p>On 10 May 1873, the first volunteer, Father Damien, arrived at the isolated settlement at Kalaupapa, where there were then 600 lepers,<sup id="cite_ref-Boeynaems_9-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boeynaems-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> and was presented by Bishop Louis Maigret. At his arrival, he spoke to the assembled lepers as "one who will be a father to you, and who loves you so much that he does not hesitate to become one of you; to live and die with you."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Damien worked with them to build a church and establish the Parish of Saint <a href="/wiki/Philomena" title="Philomena">Philomena</a>. In addition to serving as a priest, he dressed residents' ulcers, built a reservoir, built homes and furniture, made coffins, and dug graves.<sup id="cite_ref-colp_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-colp-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Six months after his arrival at Kalawao, he wrote to his brother, Pamphile, in Europe: "...I make myself a leper with the lepers to gain all to <a href="/wiki/Jesus_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Jesus Christ">Jesus Christ</a>."<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>During this time, Father Damien cared for the lepers and established leaders within the community to improve the state of living. Father Damien aided the colony by teaching, painting houses, organizing farms, and organizing the construction of chapels, roads, hospitals, and churches. He also dressed residents, dug graves, built coffins, ate food by hand with lepers, shared pipes with them, and lived with the lepers as equals. Father Damien also served as a priest during this time and spread the Catholic faith to the lepers; it is said that Father Damien told the lepers that despite what the outside world thought of them, they were always precious in the eyes of God.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir,_at_Kalaupapa,_Molokai,_circa_1878.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir%2C_at_Kalaupapa%2C_Molokai%2C_circa_1878.jpg/220px-Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir%2C_at_Kalaupapa%2C_Molokai%2C_circa_1878.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir%2C_at_Kalaupapa%2C_Molokai%2C_circa_1878.jpg/330px-Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir%2C_at_Kalaupapa%2C_Molokai%2C_circa_1878.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir%2C_at_Kalaupapa%2C_Molokai%2C_circa_1878.jpg/440px-Father_Damien_with_the_Kalawao_Girls_Choir%2C_at_Kalaupapa%2C_Molokai%2C_circa_1878.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4077" data-file-height="2731" /></a><figcaption>Father Damien, seen here with the Kalawao Girls Choir during the 1870s.</figcaption></figure><p>Some historians believed that Father Damien was a catalyst for a turning point for the community. Under his leadership, basic laws were enforced, shacks were upgraded and improved as painted houses, working farms were organized, and schools were established. At his request and of the lepers, Father Damien remained on Moloka&#x2bb;i.<sup id="cite_ref-Tayman_4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tayman-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Many such accounts, however, overlook the roles of superintendents who were Hawaiian or part-Hawaiian. Pennie Moblo states that until the late 20th century, most historical reports of Damien's ministry revealed biases of Europeans and Americans, and nearly completely discounted the roles of the native residents on Moloka&#x2bb;i.<sup id="cite_ref-critic_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-critic-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> However, it could be asserted that Pennie Moblo does not account for the separation of civil authorities and religious authorities. As was customary in the time period, Fr. Damien's work was reported to Europeans and Americans in order to raise funds for the mission. How the colony was governed would be outside the scope of the written accounts and not important to raise funds for the charitable works of Father Damien.
</p><h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Recognition_during_his_lifetime">Recognition during his lifetime</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Recognition during his lifetime">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Kal%C4%81kaua" title="Kalākaua">King David Kalākaua</a> bestowed on Damien the honor of "Knight Commander of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Order_of_Kal%C4%81kaua" title="Royal Order of Kalākaua">Royal Order of Kalākaua</a>."<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> When Crown Princess Lydia <a href="/wiki/Liliuokalani" class="mw-redirect" title="Liliuokalani">Lili&#x2bb;uokalani</a> visited the settlement to present the medal, she was reported as having been too distraught and heartbroken at the sight of the residents to read her speech. The princess shared her experience, acclaiming Damien's efforts.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Consequently, Damien became internationally known in the United States and Europe. American <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestants</a> raised large sums of money for the missionary's work. The <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> sent food, medicine, clothing, and supplies to the settlement. It is believed that Damien never wore the royal medal, although it was placed by his side during his funeral.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Illness_and_death">Illness and death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Illness and death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow{display:flex;flex-direction:row;clear:left;flex-wrap:wrap;width:100%;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .tsingle{margin:1px;float:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output Damien's funeral <a href="/wiki/Bier" title="Bier">bier</a> (image reversed)</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:222px;max-width:222px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Fatherdamiengrave.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Fatherdamiengrave.jpg/220px-Fatherdamiengrave.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/2b/Fatherdamiengrave.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="320" data-file-height="247" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">The <a href="/wiki/Leprosy" title="Leprosy">leprosy</a> patients of Moloka&#x2bb;i gathered around Father Damien's grave in mourning</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Father Damien worked in Hawaii for 16 years, providing comfort to the lepers of Kalaupapa. In addition to giving the people faith, he built homes for them and he treated them with his medical expertise. He prayed at the cemetery of the deceased and he also comforted the dying at their bedsides.
</p><p>In December 1884, while he was preparing to bathe, Damien inadvertently put his foot into scalding water, causing his skin to blister. He felt nothing and realized that he had contracted leprosy after working in the colony for 11 years.<sup id="cite_ref-Tayman_4-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Tayman-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> This was a common way for people to discover that they had been infected with leprosy. Despite his illness, Damien worked even harder.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1885, <a href="/wiki/Masanao_Goto" title="Masanao Goto">Masanao Goto</a>, a Japanese leprologist, came to <a href="/wiki/Honolulu" title="Honolulu">Honolulu</a> and treated Damien. He believed that leprosy was caused by a diminution of the blood. His treatment consisted of nourishing foods, moderate exercise, frequent friction to the benumbed parts, special ointments, and medical baths. The treatments relieved some of the symptoms and they were very popular with the Hawaiian patients as a result. Damien had faith in the treatments and said that he only wanted to be treated by Goto,<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> who eventually became a good friend of Father Damien.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite the fact that the illness was slowing his body down, Damien engaged in a flurry of activities during his last years. With his remaining time, he tried to advance and complete as many projects as possible. While he was continuing to spread the Catholic Faith and aid the lepers during their treatments, Damien completed several building projects and improved orphanages. Four volunteers arrived at Kalaupapa to help the ailing missionary: a Belgian priest, Louis Lambert Conrardy; a soldier, <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Dutton" title="Joseph Dutton">Joseph Dutton</a> (an <a href="/wiki/American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War">American Civil War</a> veteran who left behind a marriage which had been broken by his <a href="/wiki/Alcoholism" title="Alcoholism">alcoholism</a>); a male nurse from <a href="/wiki/Chicago" title="Chicago">Chicago</a>, James Sinnett; and Mother (now Saint) <a href="/wiki/Marianne_Cope" title="Marianne Cope">Marianne Cope</a>, who had been the head of the <a href="/wiki/Franciscan" class="mw-redirect" title="Franciscan">Franciscan</a>-run St Joseph's Hospital in <a href="/wiki/Syracuse,_New_York" title="Syracuse, New York">Syracuse, New York</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-damien_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-damien-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Conrardy took up Damien's pastoral duties. Cope organized a working hospital. Dutton attended to the construction and maintenance of the community's buildings. Sinnett nursed Damien during the last phases of his illness.
</p><p>With an arm in a sling, with a foot in bandages, and with his leg dragging, Damien knew that his death was near. He was bedridden on 23 March 1889, and on 30 March, he made a general confession.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Damien died of leprosy at 8:00&#160;a.m. on 15 April 1889, at the age of 49.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The next day, after the <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Mass</a> was said by Father Moellers at St. Philomena's, the whole settlement followed the funeral cortège to the cemetery. Damien was laid to rest under the same <a href="/wiki/Pandanus" title="Pandanus">pandanus</a> tree where he first slept upon his arrival on Moloka&#x2bb;i.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In January 1936, at the request of King <a href="/wiki/Leopold_III_of_Belgium" title="Leopold III of Belgium">Leopold III of Belgium</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Belgium" title="Politics of Belgium">Belgian government</a>, Damien's body was returned to his native land in Belgium. It was transported aboard the Belgian ship <i><a href="/wiki/Mercator_(ship)" title="Mercator (ship)">Mercator</a>.</i> Damien was buried in Leuven, the historic university city which is close to the village where he was born. After Damien's beatification in June 1995, the remains of his right hand were returned to Hawaii and re-interred in his original grave on Moloka&#x2bb;i.<sup id="cite_ref-starbulletin.com_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-starbulletin.com-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Commentary_after_his_death">Commentary after his death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Commentary after his death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Father Damien had become internationally known before his death, because he was seen as a symbolic Christian figure who spent his life caring for the afflicted natives. His superiors thought that Damien lacked education and finesse but they considered him to be "an earnest peasant hard at work in his own way for God."<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> News of his death on 15 April was quickly carried across the globe by the modern communications of the time, by steamship to Honolulu and California, telegraph to the East Coast of the United States, and cable to England, reaching London on 11 May.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Following an outpouring of praise for his work, other voices began to be heard in Hawai&#x2bb;i.
</p><p>Representatives of the <a href="/wiki/Congregational_church" title="Congregational church">Congregational</a> and <a href="/wiki/Presbyterianism" title="Presbyterianism">Presbyterian</a> churches in Hawaii criticized his approach. Reverend <a href="/wiki/Charles_McEwen_Hyde" title="Charles McEwen Hyde">Charles McEwen Hyde</a>, a Presbyterian minister in Honolulu, wrote to his fellow pastor Reverend H. B. Gage of San Francisco in August. Hyde referred to Father Damien as "a coarse, dirty man," who contracted leprosy due to "carelessness."<sup id="cite_ref-letter_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Hyde said that Damien was mistakenly being given credit for reforms which had actually been implemented by the Board of Health. Without consulting Hyde, Gage had the letter published in a San Francisco newspaper, generating comment and controversy in the US and Hawai&#x2bb;i. The white people of the period consistently overlooked the role which had been played by the Hawaiians themselves, among whom were several who had prominent leadership positions on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Later in 1889, the <a href="/wiki/Scottish_people" title="Scottish people">Scottish</a> author <a href="/wiki/Robert_Louis_Stevenson" title="Robert Louis Stevenson">Robert Louis Stevenson</a> and his family arrived in Hawaii for an extended stay. He had <a href="/wiki/Tuberculosis" title="Tuberculosis">tuberculosis</a>, a disease which was also considered incurable, and he was seeking some relief for it. Moved by Damien's story, he became interested in the priest's controversy and went to Moloka&#x2bb;i for eight days and seven nights.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> Stevenson wanted to learn more about Damien at the place where he had worked. He spoke with residents of various religious backgrounds in order to learn more about Damien's work. Based on his conversations and observations, he wrote an <a href="/wiki/Open_letter" title="Open letter">open letter</a> to Hyde in which he addressed the minister's criticisms and he had it printed at his own expense. Stevenson's letter became the most famous account of Damien, featuring him in the role of a European who was aiding a benighted native people.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_37-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Daws_1984_p._14_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Daws_1984_p._14-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his "6,000-word polemic,"<sup id="cite_ref-Daws_1984_p._14_40-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Daws_1984_p._14-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> Stevenson praised Damien extensively, writing to Hyde:
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>If that world at all remember you, on the day when Damien of Molokai shall be named a Saint, it will be in virtue of one work: your letter to the Reverend H. B. Gage.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_37-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Stevenson referred to his journal entries in his letter:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>...I have set down these private passages, as you perceive, without correction; thanks to you, the public has them in their bluntness. They are almost a list of the man's faults, for it is rather these that I was seeking: with his virtues, with the heroic profile of his life, I and the world were already sufficiently acquainted. I was besides a little suspicious of Catholic testimony, in no ill sense, but merely because Damien's admirers and disciples were the least likely to be critical. I know you will be more suspicious still, and the facts set down above were one and all collected from the lips of Protestants who had opposed the father in his life. Yet I am strangely deceived, or they build up the image of a man, with all his weakness, essentially heroic, and alive with rugged honesty, generosity, and mirth.<sup id="cite_ref-letter_37-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-letter-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Since then, historians and <a href="/wiki/Ethnology" title="Ethnology">ethnologists</a> have also studied Damien's work and residents' lives on Moloka&#x2bb;i. For example, Pennie Moblo, in researching the myth and the controversy surrounding the priest, has concluded they did not develop from the religious conflict between Protestants and Catholics, but instead from changes in relationships on Hawai&#x2bb;i between and among the royalty, European-American planters, missionaries, and native Hawaiians in the years leading up to the <a href="/wiki/Overthrow_of_the_Hawaiian_Kingdom" title="Overthrow of the Hawaiian Kingdom">overthrow of the Hawaiian Kingdom</a> and the U.S. takeover.<sup id="cite_ref-moblo_17-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-moblo-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>  During this period, Damien had J.K. Kahuila, a Hawaiian Protestant minister in his care, put in irons and deported to Oahu because he believed the man was too rebellious. Kahuila got a lawyer and demanded an investigation of Damien.<sup id="cite_ref-moblo_17-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-moblo-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Moblo concludes that in most 19th- and 20th-century accounts, "the focus on Damien eclipses the active role played by Hawaiians and preserves a colonially biased history."<sup id="cite_ref-moblo_17-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-moblo-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mohandas_Karamchand_Gandhi" class="mw-redirect" title="Mohandas Karamchand Gandhi">Mahatma Gandhi</a> said that Father Damien's work had inspired his social campaigns in India, leading to the independence of his people and the securing of aid for needy Indians. Gandhi was quoted in T.N. Jagadisan's 1965 publication <i>Mahatma Gandhi Answers the Challenge of Leprosy</i>:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The political and journalistic world can boast of very few heroes who compare with Father Damien of Molokai. The Catholic Church, on the contrary, counts by the thousands those who, after the example of Fr. Damien, have devoted themselves to the victims of leprosy. It is worthwhile to look for the sources of such heroism.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Canonization">Canonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Canonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:224px;max-width:224px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:222px;max-width:222px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Father_Damien_grave.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Father_Damien_grave.jpg/220px-Father_Damien_grave.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Father_Damien_grave.jpg/330px-Father_Damien_grave.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Father_Damien_grave.jpg/440px-Father_Damien_grave.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1136" data-file-height="852" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Original grave of Father Damien next to the St. Philomena Roman Catholic Church in Kalawao, Kalaupapa Peninsula, Moloka&#x2bb;i, Hawaii (<span class="geo-inline"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1156832818">.mw-parser-output .geo-default,.mw-parser-output .geo-dms,.mw-parser-output .geo-dec{display:inline}.mw-parser-output .geo-nondefault,.mw-parser-output .geo-multi-punct,.mw-parser-output .geo-inline-hidden{display:none}.mw-parser-output .longitude,.mw-parser-output .latitude{white-space:nowrap}</style><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Father_Damien&amp;params=21_10_37_N_156_56_53.3_W_region:US-HI_type:landmark"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">21°10′37″N</span> <span class="longitude">156°56′53.3″W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">21.17694°N 156.948139°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">21.17694; -156.948139</span></span></span></a></span></span>)</div></div></div><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:222px;max-width:222px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG/220px-Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG/330px-Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG/440px-Paterdamiaan_graf.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption">Grave of Saint Damien in the crypt of the church of the Congregation of the Sacred Hearts in <a href="/wiki/Leuven" title="Leuven">Leuven</a>, Belgium (<span class="geo-inline"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1156832818"><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="https://geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Father_Damien&amp;params=50_52_33.4_N_004_41_54.1_E_region:BE-VBR_type:landmark"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">50°52′33.4″N</span> <span class="longitude">004°41′54.1″E</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">50.875944°N 4.698361°E</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">50.875944; 4.698361</span></span></span></a></span></span>)</div></div></div></div></div>
<p>In 1977, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> declared Father Damien to be <a href="/wiki/Venerable" class="mw-redirect" title="Venerable">venerable</a>. On 4 June 1995, <a href="/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II" title="Pope John Paul II">Pope John Paul II</a> beatified him, by which he would be known by the official spiritual title of Blessed. On 20 December 1999, <a href="/wiki/Jorge_Medina_Est%C3%A9vez" class="mw-redirect" title="Jorge Medina Estévez">Jorge Medina Estévez</a>, Prefect of the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_Divine_Worship_and_the_Discipline_of_the_Sacraments" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for Divine Worship and the Discipline of the Sacraments">Congregation for Divine Worship and the Discipline of the Sacraments</a>, confirmed the November 1999 decision of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Conference_of_Catholic_Bishops" title="United States Conference of Catholic Bishops">United States Conference of Catholic Bishops</a> to include Blessed Damien in the national <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_year" title="Liturgical year">liturgical calendar</a> with the rank of an optional memorial. Father Damien was canonized on 11 October 2009 by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a>. His feast day is celebrated on 10 May. In Hawaii, it is celebrated on the day of his death, 15 April.
</p><p>Prior to his beatification, two <a href="/wiki/Miracles" class="mw-redirect" title="Miracles">miracles</a> were attributed to Father Damien's posthumous intercession. On 13 June 1992, Pope John Paul II approved the cure of a religious sister in France in 1895 as a miracle attributed to Venerable Damien's <a href="/wiki/Intercession" title="Intercession">intercession</a>. In that case, Sister Simplicia Hue began a <a href="/wiki/Novena" title="Novena">novena</a> to Father Damien as she lay dying of a lingering intestinal illness. It is stated that the pain and symptoms of the illness disappeared overnight.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the second case, Audrey Toguchi, a Hawaiian woman who suffered from a rare form of cancer, had remission after having prayed at the grave of Father Damien on Moloka&#x2bb;i. There was no medical explanation, as her prognosis was terminal.<sup id="cite_ref-bernardo_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bernardo-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-miracle_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-miracle-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> In 1997, Toguchi was diagnosed with <a href="/wiki/Liposarcoma" title="Liposarcoma">liposarcoma</a>, a cancer that arises in fat cells. She underwent surgery a year later and a tumor was removed, but the cancer metastasized to her lungs. Her physician, Dr. Walter Chang, told her, "Nobody has ever survived this cancer. It's going to take you."<sup id="cite_ref-bernardo_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bernardo-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> Toguchi was still alive in 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 2008, the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a> accepted the two cures as evidence of Father Damien's sanctity. On 2 June 2008, the <a href="/wiki/Congregation_for_the_Causes_of_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregation for the Causes of Saints">Congregation for the Causes of Saints</a> voted to recommend raising Father Damien of Moloka&#x2bb;i to sainthood. The decree that officially notes and verifies the miracle needed for canonization was approved by Pope Benedict XVI and promulgated by <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_(Catholic_Church)" title="Cardinal (Catholic Church)">Cardinal</a> <a href="/wiki/Jos%C3%A9_Saraiva_Martins" title="José Saraiva Martins">José Saraiva Martins</a> on 3 July 2008, with the actual ceremony of beatification taking place in Rome and celebrations in Belgium and Hawaii.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> On 21 February 2009, the Holy See announced that Father Damien would be canonized.<sup id="cite_ref-CNA_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CNA-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> The ceremony of canonization took place in Rome on Sunday, 11 October 2009, in the presence of <a href="/wiki/Albert_II_of_Belgium" title="Albert II of Belgium">King Albert II of the Belgians</a> and <a href="/wiki/Queen_Paola_of_Belgium" title="Queen Paola of Belgium">Queen Paola</a> as well as the <a href="/wiki/Belgian_Prime_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="Belgian Prime Minister">Belgian Prime Minister</a>, <a href="/wiki/Herman_Van_Rompuy" title="Herman Van Rompuy">Herman Van Rompuy</a>, and several cabinet ministers,<sup id="cite_ref-VR_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VR-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>. In Washington, D.C., President <a href="/wiki/Barack_Obama" title="Barack Obama">Barack Obama</a> affirmed his deep admiration for St. Damien, saying that he gave voice to the voiceless and dignity to the sick.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Four other individuals were canonized with Father Damien that the same day: <a href="/wiki/Zygmunt_Szcz%C4%99sny_Feli%C5%84ski" title="Zygmunt Szczęsny Feliński">Zygmunt Szczęsny Feliński</a>, Sister <a href="/wiki/Jeanne_Jugan" title="Jeanne Jugan">Jeanne Jugan</a>, Father <a href="/wiki/Francisco_Coll_Guitart" title="Francisco Coll Guitart">Francisco Coll Guitart</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rafael_Arn%C3%A1iz_Bar%C3%B3n" class="mw-redirect" title="Rafael Arnáiz Barón">Rafael Arnáiz Barón</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-nytimes_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nytimes-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Damien is honored, together with <a href="/wiki/Marianne_Cope" title="Marianne Cope">Marianne Cope</a>, with a feast day on the <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Episcopal_Church_in_the_United_States_of_America)" class="mw-redirect" title="Calendar of saints (Episcopal Church in the United States of America)">liturgical calendar of the Episcopal Church (USA)</a> on 15 April.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_arts_and_media">In arts and media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: In arts and media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:FrDamienSm.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/FrDamienSm.jpg/170px-FrDamienSm.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="277" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/14/FrDamienSm.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="175" data-file-height="285" /></a><figcaption>This <a href="/wiki/Reredos" title="Reredos">reredos</a> of Father Damien in the Episcopal <a href="/w/index.php?title=St._Thomas_the_Apostle_Hollywood&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="St. Thomas the Apostle Hollywood (page does not exist)">St. Thomas the Apostle Hollywood</a> shows cross-denominational <a href="/wiki/Veneration" title="Veneration">veneration</a> of the priest.</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Films">Films</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Films">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>Director <a href="/wiki/David_Miller_(director)" title="David Miller (director)">David Miller</a> made a <a href="/wiki/Short_film" title="Short film">short film</a> about Father Damien's life entitled <i><a href="/wiki/The_Great_Heart" title="The Great Heart">The Great Heart</a></i> (1938), released by <a href="/wiki/Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer" title="Metro-Goldwyn-Mayer">MGM</a>.</li>
<li>The first <a href="/wiki/Feature_film" title="Feature film">full-length</a> film about Father Damien was <i>Molokai</i> (1959), a Spanish production which was directed by <a href="/wiki/Luis_Lucia" title="Luis Lucia">Luis Lucia</a> with Javier Escrivá, <a href="/wiki/Roberto_Camardiel" title="Roberto Camardiel">Roberto Camardiel</a>, and <a href="/wiki/G%C3%A9rard_Tichy" title="Gérard Tichy">Gérard Tichy</a> playing the main roles.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ken_Howard" title="Ken Howard">Ken Howard</a> played the title role in the television film <i>Father Damien: The Leper Priest</i> (1980);<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> he replaced <a href="/wiki/David_Janssen" title="David Janssen">David Janssen</a>, who died suddenly after several days of shooting.</li>
<li>Stephanie J. Castillo's documentary <i>Simple Courage</i> (1992) explores Damien and his work, drawing parallels between the treatment of persons who have leprosy and the stigma which is associated with persons who have <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a>. "Simple Courage" was rewarded an EMMY Award in 1993.<sup id="cite_ref-critic_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-critic-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The Belgian <a href="/wiki/Film_producer" title="Film producer">film producer</a> Tharsi Vanhuysse produced and <a href="/wiki/Paul_Cox_(director)" title="Paul Cox (director)">Paul Cox</a> directed the film <i><a href="/wiki/Molokai:_The_Story_of_Father_Damien" title="Molokai: The Story of Father Damien">Molokai: The Story of Father Damien</a></i> (1999) with <a href="/wiki/David_Wenham" title="David Wenham">David Wenham</a> as Damien.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template noprint noexcerpt Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:NOTRS" class="mw-redirect" title="Wikipedia:NOTRS"><span title="This claim needs references to better sources. (May 2020)">better&#160;source&#160;needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li>
<li>Interviews which were conducted by former residents are featured in the documentary <i>The Soul of Kalaupapa: Voices of Exile</i> (2011).<sup id="cite_ref-8thcircle_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8thcircle-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> It focuses on the efforts of Belgian-born Father Damien in the 19th century and the efforts of <a href="/wiki/Jonatana_Napela" title="Jonatana Napela">Jonatana Napela</a>, a Hawaiian <a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">LDS</a> convert who works with persons with leprosy in Kalaupapa and collaborates on ecumenical efforts.<sup id="cite_ref-8thcircle_54-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8thcircle-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Splendor Producciones, an Argentinian amateur film-making group, produced a movie about Father Damien's full life which is titled <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Coraz%C3%B3n_de_H%C3%A9roe:_Padre_Dami%C3%A1n_de_Veuster&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Corazón de Héroe: Padre Damián de Veuster (page does not exist)">Corazón de Héroe: Padre Damián de Veuster</a></i> (2021)<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>Screenwriter and film director <a href="/wiki/John_Farrow" title="John Farrow">John Farrow</a> wrote the biography <i>Damien the Leper</i> (1937).<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> In 1939, <a href="/wiki/RKO_Pictures" title="RKO Pictures">RKO Pictures</a> purchased the book for a feature film titled <i>Father Damien</i>, to be directed by Farrow and star <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Calleia" title="Joseph Calleia">Joseph Calleia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> The project was not realized.</li>
<li>The poetic dramatization <i>Father Damien</i> (1938) was written by Edward Snelson, later Joint Secretary to the Government of India (1947), KBE, and dedicated 'To G.,' the actress Greer Garson, to whom he had been married in 1933.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The one-person play <i><a href="/wiki/Damien_(play)" title="Damien (play)">Damien</a></i> by Aldyth Morris was broadcast nationally on PBS in the United States in 1978 and again in 1986 on "American Playhouse." The broadcast received several recognitions, including a Peabody Award.</li>
<li>The 2016 novel <i>God Made Us Monsters</i> by William Neary explores Father Damien's rise to sainthood.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Monuments_and_statues">Monuments and statues</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Monuments and statues">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Painted_Church_127.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Painted_Church_127.jpg/220px-Painted_Church_127.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Painted_Church_127.jpg/330px-Painted_Church_127.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9a/Painted_Church_127.jpg/440px-Painted_Church_127.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="900" /></a><figcaption>Monument at St. Benedict's Catholic Church in Honaunau (Hawaii)</figcaption></figure>
<ul><li>The <a href="/wiki/Father_Damien_Statue" class="mw-redirect" title="Father Damien Statue">Father Damien Statue</a> on the steps of the <a href="/wiki/Hawaii_State_Capitol" title="Hawaii State Capitol">State Capitol Building</a> honors him, and a replica is displayed in the <a href="/wiki/National_Statuary_Hall_Collection" title="National Statuary Hall Collection">National Statuary Hall Collection</a> in the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Capitol" title="United States Capitol">United States Capitol</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-aocgov_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aocgov-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Statues in memory of Damien can be found in many Belgian cathedrals, such as the <a href="/wiki/Tournai_Cathedral" title="Tournai Cathedral">Tournai Cathedral</a>, <a href="/wiki/St_Bavo%27s_Cathedral,_Ghent" title="St Bavo&#39;s Cathedral, Ghent">St Bavo's Cathedral, Ghent</a>, and <a href="/wiki/St_Martin%27s_Cathedral,_Ypres" title="St Martin&#39;s Cathedral, Ypres">St Martin's Cathedral, Ypres</a>.</li>
<li>A monument stands in front of <a href="/wiki/St._Benedict%27s_Catholic_Church_(Honaunau,_Hawaii)" title="St. Benedict&#39;s Catholic Church (Honaunau, Hawaii)">St. Benedict's Catholic Church in Honaunau (Hawaii)</a> and is often decorated with <a href="/wiki/Lei_(garland)" title="Lei (garland)">Leis</a>.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy_and_honors">Legacy and honors</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Father_Damien&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Legacy and honors">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg/170px-Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg" decoding="async" width="170" height="223" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg/255px-Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg/340px-Statue_of_Father_Damien_3.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="1340" data-file-height="1755" /></a><figcaption>Statue outside the <a href="/wiki/Hawaii_State_Capitol" title="Hawaii State Capitol">Hawaii State Capitol</a> Building</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 2005, Damien was honored with the title of <i><a href="/wiki/De_Grootste_Belg" title="De Grootste Belg">De Grootste Belg</a></i>, chosen as "The Greatest Belgian" throughout <a href="/wiki/History_of_Belgium" title="History of Belgium">that country's history</a>, in polling conducted by the Flemish public broadcasting service, <a href="/wiki/Vlaamse_Radio-_en_Televisieomroep" class="mw-redirect" title="Vlaamse Radio- en Televisieomroep">VRT</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-canvas_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-canvas-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> He ranked third on <i><a href="/wiki/Le_plus_grand_Belge" class="mw-redirect" title="Le plus grand Belge">Le plus grand Belge</a></i> ("The Greatest Belgian") in a poll by the French-speaking public channel <a href="/wiki/RTBF" title="RTBF">RTBF</a>.
</p><p>In 1952, the Picpus Fathers (SS .CC) opened the <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://damiaanmuseum.be/en/">Damien Museum</a>, (Dutch: <a href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damiaanmuseum" class="extiw" title="nl:Damiaanmuseum">Damiaanmuseum</a> ) in Tremelo, Belgium, in the house where Damien was born and grew up. In 2017, the museum was completely renovated.
</p><p>With his canonization highlighting his ministry to persons with leprosy, Father Damien's work has been cited as an example of how society should minister to <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS" title="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</a> patients.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> On the occasion of Damien's canonization, President <a href="/wiki/Barack_Obama" title="Barack Obama">Barack Obama</a> stated, "In our own time, as millions around the world suffer from disease, especially the pandemic of HIV/AIDS, we should draw on the example of Father Damien’s resolve in answering the urgent call to heal and care for the sick."<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Several clinics and centers nationwide catering to HIV/AIDS patients bear his name.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> There is a chapel named for him and dedicated to people with HIV/AIDS, in <a href="/w/index.php?title=St._Thomas_the_Apostle_Hollywood&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="St. Thomas the Apostle Hollywood (page does not exist)">St. Thomas the Apostle Hollywood</a>, an Episcopal parish.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Damien The Leper Society is among charities named after him that work to treat and control leprosy. Damien House, Ireland, is a centre for "peace for families and individuals affected by bereavement, stress, violence, and other difficulties with particular attention to <a href="/wiki/Northern_Ireland" title="Northern Ireland">Northern Ireland</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Saint Damien Advocates is a religious freedom organization that says it wants to carry on Father Damien's work with orphans and others.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Schools which are named after him include <a href="/wiki/Damien_High_School" title="Damien High School">Damien High School</a> in <a href="/wiki/Southern_California" title="Southern California">Southern California</a>, Saint Damien Elementary School in <a href="/wiki/Calgary,_Canada" class="mw-redirect" title="Calgary, Canada">Calgary, Canada</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Damien_Memorial_School" title="Damien Memorial School">Damien Memorial School</a> in Hawaii.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>St. Damien of Moloka&#x2bb;i Catholic Church in <a href="/wiki/Edmond,_Oklahoma" title="Edmond, Oklahoma">Edmond, Oklahoma</a>, dedicated in 2010, is believed to have been the first Roman Catholic church in the continental United States to be named for Saint Damien after his canonization. A Traditional Latin Mass church, it is operated by the <a href="/wiki/Priestly_Fraternity_of_St._Peter" class="mw-redirect" title="Priestly Fraternity of St. Peter">Priestly Fraternity of St. Peter</a> (FSSP) and was authorized in 2010 by <a href="/wiki/Eusebius_J._Beltran" title="Eusebius J. Beltran">Eusebius J. Beltran</a>, Archbishop of Oklahoma City. <a href="/wiki/Pontiac,_Michigan" title="Pontiac, Michigan">Pontiac, Michigan</a> (in the Catholic archdiocese of Detroit) has a St. Damien parish.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Marianne_of_Moloka%CA%BBi" class="mw-redirect" title="Marianne of Molokaʻi">Marianne of Molokaʻi</a> was canonized in 2012.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
